## Phase 3 Task 3.4 (2026-09-03, Claude, TDD, in this worktree/branch)

- **Status**: `DONE`. Branch `claude/awayphotoraweditor-parity-phase2-geometry`, product+test commit `d55821d`, one commit ahead of the Task 3.3 independent-review docs commit `a785205`. Base branch `main` at `fb7109a4fd76035bb9ca3f492b1fa45f511a60ec`, unchanged.
- **Scope**: Phase 3 roadmap Task 3.4 ("Compound batch undo" — full success, partial failure, and undo-after-partial-failure tests; no target sidecar left half-written; localized "affected N, failed M, skipped K" report copy). Read directly from `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-02-awayphotoraweditor-parity-roadmap.md`'s Phase 3 section before starting. `BatchAdjustmentTransaction`'s own doc comment (written during Task 3.3) had already anticipated the mechanism — "useful both as an audit trail and as the basis for Task 3.4's compound batch undo (revert = write `before[id]` back)" — so no new data model was needed, only the revert operation itself. No virtual copy (Task 3.5).
- **What changed**:
  - `BatchAdjustmentSyncService.undo(_:)` (new) — reverts a `BatchAdjustmentTransaction`: every target it actually wrote to (`results[target] == .success`) gets `before[target]` merged back onto its *current* adjustments via the same `.merge` mode `commitGesture` uses, not a blind overwrite — anything a target picked up after the original sync (to a field the sync never touched) survives the undo exactly as it survived the sync. A target the original sync never wrote to is `skipped`, never touched. One target's revert failing doesn't stop the rest, tallied into a new `BatchAdjustmentSyncService.BatchUndoSummary` (`affected`/`failed`/`skipped`) rather than throwing — same fault-tolerance shape `PresetRestoreSummary`/`restorePresets(_:into:conflict:)` already established for a multi-document restore.
  - `LibraryViewModel.lastBatchTransaction` (new, published) — the most recent batch sync that actually changed something, kept for exactly one undo; set in `endBatchGesture` (only when `modifiedFieldIDs` is non-empty, so a no-op drag/reset never clobbers a real, pending-undo transaction with an empty one), cleared by `undoLastBatchTransaction()` regardless of outcome — a compound batch undo is one-shot, not its own stack.
  - `LibraryViewModel.undoLastBatchTransaction()` (new) — calls `batchSyncService.undo(_:)`, then refreshes the grid's edit badge for every target the original sync wrote to from the *ground truth left on disk* (a fresh `services.loadAdjustments` read, checking `.isNeutral`) rather than assuming the revert made it neutral.
  - `LibraryViewModel.batchUndoSummaryMessage(_:)` (new, `nonisolated static`) — "affected N, failed M, skipped K" report copy, following the same additive-parts convention (skip a zero count, join the rest in order) `PresetBrowserView.restoreSummaryMessage` already established for `PresetRestoreSummary`.
  - `LumaHarborCommands` — new "Undo Batch Sync" menu item in the Photo menu, next to "Reset All Adjustments"; disabled whenever `lastBatchTransaction == nil`; on success shows a "Batch Sync Undone" alert with the report copy.
  - New localized strings ("Undo Batch Sync", "Batch Sync Undone", "reverted", "Nothing to undo.") landed in both `en.lproj` and `zh-Hant.lproj`; reused the existing "failed"/"skipped" keys rather than duplicating them.
- **TDD**: RED confirmed at every layer via genuine compile failures (new symbols simply absent). `BatchAdjustmentSyncServiceTests` +5 (`undo(_:)` against the not-yet-existing method: full success, a target the original sync had already failed on being skipped rather than touched, a revert write itself failing, and a no-modified-fields transaction being a no-op). `BatchUndoSummaryMessageTests` (new file, 4 tests) against the not-yet-existing `batchUndoSummaryMessage(_:)` — deliberately locale-agnostic (numbers and ordering only), since `L10n.bundle` is a `static let` resolved once from the real system language the first time any test in the process calls `L10n.t` (documented in `Sources/Localization/L10n.swift`), so an exact-English-copy assertion here would be flaky depending on the machine's system language; `LocalizationSmokeTest` is what actually pins a language deterministically via `L10n.resolveBundle(preferences:)`, and is where the Chinese-translation coverage for these new keys lives instead. `BatchAdjustmentGestureIntegrationTests` +2 (full undo-reverts-and-clears-the-transaction path end to end through `LibraryViewModel`, and a "nothing to undo" no-op) — passed immediately once the unit-level pieces were wired, no additional seam-wiring gap found this round. `LibraryGridMultiSelectContractTests` +1 (source-text-contract, matching this file's own established style) against the not-yet-written "Undo Batch Sync" menu wiring. `LocalizationSmokeTest` +1 method (4 keys).
- **Tests added**: `BatchAdjustmentSyncServiceTests` +5. `BatchUndoSummaryMessageTests` (new file, 4). `BatchAdjustmentGestureIntegrationTests` +2. `LibraryGridMultiSelectContractTests` +1. `LocalizationSmokeTest` +1 method (4 keys).
- **Verification**: full `swift test` — 1450 executed (up from 1438), 9 skipped (unchanged `RawFixtureTests` fixture-dependent baseline), 0 failures. `git diff --check` — clean. Privacy scan (all 10 changed/new files individually) — no hits. `swift build` — clean. `xcodebuild -project Apps/LumaHarborPad.xcodeproj -scheme LumaHarborPad -destination 'generic/platform=iOS' CODE_SIGNING_ALLOWED=NO build` — `** BUILD SUCCEEDED **`. `Apps/LumaHarborPad.xcodeproj/project.pbxproj` confirmed untouched before and after.
- **`NOT RUN`**: every real-device/Simulator/human-eyes manual check — this environment cannot launch and drive the Mac app's UI, so the "Undo Batch Sync" menu item's enabled/disabled state, the alert's actual appearance, and the edit badge refreshing on real thumbnails on screen are only verified via unit and source-contract tests plus one full (but still simulated-input) integration test, never a human at a running window. Consistent with every prior Phase 2/3 UI round's own `NOT RUN` entry in this environment.
- **Deliberate, documented scope boundary — not an omission**: compound batch undo is a single dedicated "Undo Batch Sync" action operating on `lastBatchTransaction`, entirely separate from the per-photo ⌘Z undo stack (`EditorSession`/`EditHistory`) — it does not try to detect "the user just pressed ⌘Z and that happened to be the edit a batch sync was based on" and trigger automatically. This matches how `BatchAdjustmentTransaction`'s own doc comment already described the mechanism ("revert = write `before[id]` back") and keeps the two undo systems structurally independent, at the cost of requiring an explicit, separate action to undo a batch.
- **Independent review**: performed — see "Independent review of Phase 3 Task 3.4" above (one real, silent-data-loss finding, fixed; one cheap wording fix; two tracked follow-ups).
- **Next action**: Task 3.5 (virtual copy), Task 3.6 (Phase 3 verification report), or pick up the independent review's Findings 2–4 as their own small round. Do not push, merge, rebase, remove this worktree, or commit any signing setting without explicit user authorization.

## Independent review of Phase 3 Task 3.3 (2026-09-03, Claude, TDD, in this worktree/branch)

- **Status**: `DONE` (fix-only round for the review's one real, non-blocking finding). Branch `claude/awayphotoraweditor-parity-phase2-geometry`, product+test commit `166688e`, one commit ahead of Task 3.3's docs+handoff commit `b347a4b`. Base branch `main` at `fb7109a4fd76035bb9ca3f492b1fa45f511a60ec`, unchanged.
- **Review performed by**: a fresh general-purpose subagent with no prior context on this work, reviewing `git diff fc21143..HEAD -- Sources/` plus `CURRENT.md`'s Task 3.3 entry and the plan's own Task 3.3 bullets, explicitly told to verify rather than accept any "documented scope boundary" claim, following the same pattern the 3.1+3.2 independent review established.
- **Finding 1 — real, fixed**: `EditorSession.resetAdjustment(_:)`/`.resetAll()` never fired `onBeginAdjustmentGesture`/`onEndAdjustmentGesture`, even though both are reachable through the same ten basic sliders the gesture hooks are wired to (a context-menu "Reset ⟨field⟩", a double-click on the row, or "Reset All Adjustments" — see `BasicAdjustmentPanel.swift`, `LumaHarborCommands.swift`, `InspectorView.swift`). Concrete scenario: batch-select photos A/B/C, drag A's exposure to 1.5 (syncs to B/C as designed), then double-click the exposure row to reset it back to 0 — A goes to 0, but B/C stay stuck at 1.5 forever, with no user-visible sign the two have diverged. This is a real gap in what Task 3.3's own "the ten basic sliders sync" claim implies, not covered by the roadmap's literal "gesture" wording.
  - **Fix**: both methods now capture `history.current` as a baseline before resetting, fire `onBeginAdjustmentGesture`/`onEndAdjustmentGesture` around the reset exactly as a drag's `onEditingChanged` does, and skip both hooks when the reset is a no-op (nothing changed, nothing to sync) — same no-op guard every other edit path in `EditorSession` already has.
- **Findings 2–5 — real-but-minor or unconfirmed, NOT fixed this round, tracked here as known follow-ups**:
  2. Cmd-click removing the *currently open* source photo's own checkmark from `selectedPhotoIDs` does not stop it from continuing to sync changes to the rest of the batch (`beginBatchGesture` always excludes the source from `targets` regardless of whether the source itself is still "checked"). Looks like "I removed myself from the batch" to the user; functionally nothing changes. No data-loss risk — a UI-semantics clarity gap only.
  3. Batch-synced targets are written immediately on `endAdjustmentGesture()`; the source photo's own edit still goes through the existing 700ms autosave debounce. A crash/force-quit in that narrow window could leave the targets' sidecars ahead of the source's own — self-corrects on the next edit, no data corruption, just a very small, pre-existing-pattern inconsistency window.
  4. **Suspected, unconfirmed**: switching the open photo to another photo already in the batch (a plain click, not Cmd-click) updates `LibraryViewModel.selectedPhotoID` synchronously, but `EditorSession.open(...)` doesn't actually load the new photo until its sidecar read completes. A drag started in that narrow window could theoretically record the wrong `sourcePhotoID`. Not reproduced — the window is sub-millisecond on local disk; flagged for visibility given this app also supports slower external/network volumes.
  5. **Suspected, unconfirmed**: `LibraryViewModel.install(services:)` relays `onBeginAdjustmentGesture`/`onEndAdjustmentGesture` through two independently-created `Task { @MainActor in ... }` blocks rather than one synchronous call, so their relative ordering relies on Swift's informal (not language-guaranteed) same-executor FIFO behavior rather than a hard guarantee. `BatchAdjustmentGestureIntegrationTests` run repeatedly with no reordering observed; not reproduced as an actual failure.
- **TDD**: RED confirmed for the fix. `EditorSessionEditingTests` +4 (`testResetAdjustmentFiresBeginAndEndGestureHooksBracketingTheReset`, `testResetAdjustmentDoesNotFireGestureHooksWhenTheFieldIsAlreadyNeutral`, `testResetAllFiresBeginAndEndGestureHooksBracketingEveryFieldItResets`, `testResetAllDoesNotFireGestureHooksWhenAlreadyNeutral`) — all failed against the pre-fix `resetAdjustment`/`resetAll` (hooks never fired). `BatchAdjustmentGestureIntegrationTests` +1 (`testResettingASliderOnTheOpenPhotoSyncsTheResetToOtherSelectedPhotos`, full grid-selection-to-sidecar-write path) — passed immediately once the unit-level fix landed, unlike the Task 3.3 round's own integration-test seam bug; no additional wiring gap this time.
- **Tests added**: `EditorSessionEditingTests` +4. `BatchAdjustmentGestureIntegrationTests` +1.
- **Verification**: full `swift test` — 1438 executed (up from 1433), 9 skipped (unchanged `RawFixtureTests` fixture-dependent baseline), 0 failures. `git diff --check` — clean. Privacy scan (all 3 changed files individually) — no hits. `swift build` — clean. `xcodebuild -project Apps/LumaHarborPad.xcodeproj -scheme LumaHarborPad -destination 'generic/platform=iOS' CODE_SIGNING_ALLOWED=NO build` — `** BUILD SUCCEEDED **`. `Apps/LumaHarborPad.xcodeproj/project.pbxproj` confirmed untouched before and after.
- **`NOT RUN`**: real-device/Simulator/human-eyes manual check of the fixed Reset/Reset All sync — same standing limitation as every prior round in this environment.
- **Next action**: Task 3.4 (compound batch undo), or wire the remaining Color/Detail/Effects/Geometry gesture hooks (Task 3.3's own documented scope boundary, unchanged by this round). Findings 2–5 above remain open, non-blocking follow-ups. Do not push, merge, rebase, remove this worktree, or commit any signing setting without explicit user authorization.

## Phase 3 Task 3.3 (2026-09-03, Claude, TDD, in this worktree/branch)

- **Status**: `DONE`. Branch `claude/awayphotoraweditor-parity-phase2-geometry`, product+test commit `0b7890359dd3fda9980c9cd964a6770b2698e366`, one commit ahead of the independent-review fix's docs commit `fc21143`. Base branch `main` at `fb7109a4fd76035bb9ca3f492b1fa45f511a60ec`, unchanged.
- **Scope**: Phase 3 roadmap Task 3.3 ("Thumbnail multi-select and batch snapshot semantics" — gesture start snapshots selected targets; only modified field IDs are synchronized; selection changes mid-drag do not change the current batch target list; implement multi-select UI and batch sync service). Read directly from `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-02-awayphotoraweditor-parity-roadmap.md`'s Phase 3 section (including its `BatchAdjustmentTransaction` data-model sketch) before starting. Researched existing architecture via an Explore subagent first (thumbnail selection, slider gesture lifecycle, per-photo sidecar read/write, existing field-diff machinery) — confirmed **no** multi-select state, no drag-start/end signal, and no arbitrary-baseline field diff existed anywhere before this round. No compound batch undo (Task 3.4), no virtual copy (Task 3.5).
- **What changed**:
  - `AdjustmentPatch.modifiedFields(in:comparedTo:)` (new overload, `PresetCore`) — diffs two arbitrary `PhotoAdjustments` snapshots (a drag's before/after), distinct from the existing `modifiedFields(in:)`'s fixed `.neutral` comparison; the existing overload is untouched, zero regression risk to its own call sites.
  - `BatchAdjustmentSyncService`/`BatchAdjustmentTransaction`/`BatchWriteResult` (new, `PhotoLibraryCore`) — an `actor` that snapshots a batch's frozen target-`PhotoID` list and the source photo's baseline at `beginGesture` (a plain value, never a live reference to the caller's selection — this is what makes "selection changes mid-drag do not change the current batch target list" a structural guarantee, not a runtime check), then at `commitGesture` diffs and syncs only the changed fields onto each target via `PresetApplicator`'s existing `.merge` mode (reused, not reimplemented), tallying per-target success/failure into a `BatchAdjustmentTransaction` rather than stopping at the first problem. Load/save are injected closures (`Loader`/`Saver` typealiases), the same dependency-injection pattern `EditorDependencies` already established.
  - `EditorSession.beginAdjustmentGesture()`/`.endAdjustmentGesture()` (new, `EditorCore`) — fire two new optional `EditorDependencies` hooks (`onBeginAdjustmentGesture`/`onEndAdjustmentGesture`, both `nil` by default) with `history.current` at the right two moments. `EditorSession` itself stays entirely library-agnostic — it has no concept of "other selected photos," it only fires the hooks if a caller supplied them. `EditorDependencies.addingBatchGestureHooks(onBegin:onEnd:)` (new) lets `LibraryViewModel` attach its own wiring onto `AppServices.editorDependencies` without repeating every other field.
  - `AdjustmentSliderRow.onEditingChanged` (new, `AdjustmentUI`, defaults to a no-op so every existing caller keeps compiling unchanged) — wires SwiftUI's own `Slider(value:in:onEditingChanged:)`, distinct from `onChange` (which fires on every drag tick). Wired into `BasicAdjustmentPanel`'s own inline Slider (the ten basic sliders: exposure, temperature, tint, contrast, highlights, shadows, whites, blacks, vibrance, saturation) via `editor.beginAdjustmentGesture()`/`.endAdjustmentGesture()`.
  - `LibraryViewModel.selectedPhotoIDs`/`toggleMultiSelect(_:)` (new) — the batch sync target set; Cmd-click in the grid toggles membership without changing which photo is open (`selectedPhotoID`). A plain click on a photo not already in the batch starts a fresh one-photo batch; clicking one that's already in the batch just changes which photo is the sync source, leaving the rest intact. `LibraryViewModel` owns the real `BatchAdjustmentSyncService` instance, with its load/save closures routed through `services.loadAdjustments`/`services.saveAdjustments` — the same seam the currently-open photo's own autosave already uses (not `services.libraryService.adjustments(for:)`/`saveAdjustments(for:)` directly) — see the TDD note below for why this specific choice mattered, not just for style.
  - `PhotoGridCell` gained `isBatchSelected` — a checkmark badge distinct from the existing accent-color ring (`isSelected`, "open in the editor"); `LibraryGridView` reads `NSEvent.modifierFlags.contains(.command)` inside its `.onTapGesture` to distinguish a Cmd-click from a plain one (SwiftUI's own tap gesture reports no modifier flags itself).
  - New localized string ("Included in the current batch selection") landed in both `en.lproj` and `zh-Hant.lproj`.
- **Deliberate, documented scope boundary — not an omission**: only the ten basic sliders are wired to the gesture hooks this round. `AdjustmentSliderRow.onEditingChanged` is available to Color/Detail/Effects/Geometry's ~38 finer-grained fields (HSL bands, split toning, sharpening, noise reduction, vignette, grain, rotate/straighten) too, but none of those four panels' ~17 individual `AdjustmentSliderRow(...)` call sites were touched this round — wiring them is mechanical (the same three-line addition already proven in `BasicAdjustmentPanel`) but was left out to keep this round's diff reviewable. Batch sync for those finer fields needs no engine changes, only UI wiring.
- **TDD**: RED confirmed at every layer via genuine compile failures (new symbols simply absent, no stash-and-restore needed). `AdjustmentPatchExtractionTests` +4 against the not-yet-existing `modifiedFields(in:comparedTo:)`. `BatchAdjustmentSyncServiceTests` (9, new file) against the not-yet-existing service. `EditorSessionEditingTests` +4 against the not-yet-existing `beginAdjustmentGesture`/`endAdjustmentGesture`. `AdjustmentGroupPanelsContractTests`/`BasicAdjustmentPanelModelTests` +1 each (source-contract) against the not-yet-wired `onEditingChanged`. `LibraryGridMultiSelectContractTests` (2, new file, source-contract) against the not-yet-written Cmd-click/badge UI. `LocalizationSmokeTest` +1.
  - **Genuine bug caught by the end-to-end integration test, not by any layer test in isolation**: `BatchAdjustmentGestureIntegrationTests.testDraggingASliderOnTheOpenPhotoSyncsOnlyTheChangedFieldToOtherSelectedPhotos` first RAN (not just compiled) RED — a real 5-second timeout, not a fast failure — because `LibraryViewModel`'s first draft of `batchSyncService`'s load/save closures called `services.libraryService.adjustments(for:)`/`saveAdjustments(for:)` directly, bypassing the test-injectable `services.loadAdjustments`/`services.saveAdjustments` seam `AppTestSupport.makeServices(loadAdjustments:saveAdjustments:)` overrides. In production this is behaviorally identical (`AppServices.makeDefault()` wires both to the same `libraryService` call), so it would have shipped a working feature that was simply untestable through this codebase's own established test-double seam — caught only because the integration test asserted through the real `LibraryViewModel`/`EditorSession` pipeline rather than calling `BatchAdjustmentSyncService` directly. Fixed by routing through `services.loadAdjustments`/`saveAdjustments` instead; the same test then passed in 0.059s.
- **Tests added**: `AdjustmentPatchExtractionTests` +4. `BatchAdjustmentSyncServiceTests` (9, new file). `EditorSessionEditingTests` +4. `AdjustmentGroupPanelsContractTests` +1. `BasicAdjustmentPanelModelTests` +1. `LibraryGridMultiSelectContractTests` (2, new file). `BatchAdjustmentGestureIntegrationTests` (2, new file — full grid-selection-to-sidecar-write integration, seeded real files on disk through `AppViewModelTestCase`). `LocalizationSmokeTest` +1.
- **Verification**: full `swift test` — 1433 executed (up from the independent-review fix round's 1409), 9 skipped (unchanged `RawFixtureTests` fixture-dependent baseline), 0 failures. `git diff --check` — clean. Privacy scan (every one of the 19 changed/new files individually) — no hits. `swift build` — clean. `xcodebuild -project Apps/LumaHarborPad.xcodeproj -scheme LumaHarborPad -destination 'generic/platform=iOS' CODE_SIGNING_ALLOWED=NO build` — `** BUILD SUCCEEDED **` (this round's Mac-only files — `LibraryGridView.swift`, `ThumbnailView.swift`, the new `AppKit` import and `NSEvent` usage — live in the `LumaHarborApp` target, which the iPad app's own Xcode project never links; only the shared `PresetCore`/`PhotoLibraryCore`/`EditorCore`/`AdjustmentUI` changes are exercised by this gate, and all built clean). `Apps/LumaHarborPad.xcodeproj/project.pbxproj` confirmed untouched before and after.
- **`NOT RUN`**: every real-device/Simulator/human-eyes manual check — this environment cannot launch and drive the Mac app's UI, so the actual feel of a Cmd-click, the checkmark badge's appearance, and a real slider drag syncing across real thumbnails are only verified via unit and source-contract tests plus one full (but still simulated-input) integration test, never a human at a running window. Consistent with every prior round's own `NOT RUN` entry in this environment.
- **Independent review**: performed — see "Independent review of Phase 3 Task 3.3" above (one real, non-blocking finding, fixed).
- **Next action**: Task 3.4 (compound batch undo — full success/partial failure/undo-after-partial-failure, no target sidecar left half-written, localized "affected N, failed M, skipped K" report copy), or wire the remaining Color/Detail/Effects/Geometry panels' gesture hooks (documented scope boundary above). ## Independent review of Phase 3 Tasks 3.1+3.2 (2026-09-03, Claude, TDD, in this worktree/branch)

- **Status**: `DONE` (fix-only round for the review's one blocking + one real-but-minor finding). Branch `claude/awayphotoraweditor-parity-phase2-geometry`, product+test commit `917f0ef12a5768724d291cdcf79b0b5a05be679d`, one commit ahead of Task 3.2's docs commit `4f8e9a5`. Base branch `main` at `fb7109a4fd76035bb9ca3f492b1fa45f511a60ec`, unchanged.
- **Review performed by**: a fresh general-purpose subagent with no prior context on this work (genuinely independent, not this session's own self-review), reviewing `git diff 382d799..HEAD` plus both tasks' `CURRENT.md` entries, explicitly told to verify rather than accept every "documented scope boundary, not omission" claim in those entries.
- **Finding #1 — BLOCKING, fixed**: `PresetLibraryViewModel.copy(_:to:)` forwarded a built-in preset's own fixed, permanently built-in UUID (`BuiltInPresetRepository.defaultPresets`' `00000000-…-0001`/`…-0002`) straight into a real, file-backed scope on "Copy to My Presets". `FilePresetRepository.save` never finds an existing file under that UUID in the destination (built-in presets are never file-backed anywhere), so `conflict` is never even consulted — the write silently succeeds, permanently giving two distinct `PresetListItem`s (one `.builtIn`, one `.mine`) the same `Identifiable` id (`PresetListItem.id` is just `document.id`, not scope-qualified), on disk, surviving every future launch. Entirely invisible to the existing 1406-test suite: the only test on this path used `RecordingPresetRepository`, whose mock `save()` always returns `.created` regardless of `conflict`/content and was never asserted on the *saved id*, only the name.
  - **Fix**: `copy(_:to:)` now mints a fresh `id`/`createdAt`/`modifiedAt` when `item.scope == .builtIn`, the same way `.xmp`/`.lhpreset` import already does for the identical underlying reason. Deliberately scoped to `.builtIn` only — a copy between `.mine` and `.library` still preserves identity, since the repository layer's own `presetDocumentsAreCanonicallyEqual`/`.keepBoth` duplicate detection depends on that (pinned by a new regression test, `testCopyingABetweenMineAndLibraryPreservesIdentity`, confirming the fix doesn't touch that path).
- **Finding #2 — real, fixed**: `PresetLibraryViewModel.previewLHPreset(data:suggestedName:)` (the new `.lhpreset` import path from Task 3.2) built its `proposedPreset` from only `name`/`groupPath`/`patch`, silently dropping `source` and `xmpEnvelope`. Concrete loss scenario: import a Lightroom `.xmp` with an unmapped property (preserved verbatim in `xmpEnvelope.originalPacketUTF8`) → export as native `.lhpreset` to share it → recipient imports that `.lhpreset` → silently becomes `source: .native`, loses the "Imported" badge (Task 3.1) and the envelope → a later re-export to `.xmp` falls back to a blank envelope (`XMPExporter.baseDocument(for:)`), permanently losing every previously-preserved unmapped property — exactly the class of loss Task 3.2's own "preserve unknown XMP fields" work exists to prevent, just not on this particular path.
  - **Fix**: `previewLHPreset` now carries `decoded.source`/`decoded.xmpEnvelope` through into `proposedPreset` unchanged.
- **Findings #3 and #4 — real-but-minor, NOT fixed this round, tracked here as known follow-ups**:
  3. `EditPresetSheet.binding(for:)`'s `get` (`group.fields.isSubset(of: keptFields)`) requires *every* field in a multi-leaf group (HSL channels, split toning, sharpening, noise reduction, vignette, grain) to be present before showing the group's checkbox checked — so a group whose *present* fields are all still kept (e.g. only HSL Red's hue+saturation were ever set, and both are kept) renders as unchecked. No data loss (`updatePreset`'s `excluding()` only ever removes a field both present and missing from `keptFields`, so the misleading checkbox toggling "on" is a no-op) — purely a display/UX confusion risk.
  4. `PresetBrowserView`'s `exportPreset`/`backupPresets(scope:)`/`restorePresets()` are three independent `Task { }` blocks that all eventually assign to the same shared `@State private var exportError`. Triggering two of these panels before the first's async tail completes lets whichever finishes last silently overwrite the other's alert/success message — the underlying I/O for each still completes correctly and independently; only the user-facing confirmation can be dropped.
- **TDD**: RED confirmed for both fixes before implementing. `testCopyingABuiltInPresetMintsAFreshIdentityRatherThanReusingTheBuiltInsUUID` (new) failed with the built-in's own UUID `00000000-0000-0000-0000-000000000001` appearing in the saved document, against the pre-fix `copy(_:to:)`. `testPreviewImportOfALhpresetFilePreservesItsSourceAndXMPEnvelope` (new) failed with `source == .native`/`xmpEnvelope == nil` against the pre-fix `previewLHPreset`. A third new test, `testCopyingABetweenMineAndLibraryPreservesIdentity`, was written as an explicit regression guard for the *existing*, already-correct `.mine`↔`.library` behavior and passed immediately (confirming the fix's `.builtIn`-only scoping is correct, not accidentally broadened).
- **Tests added**: `PresetWorkflowTests` +3 (`testCopyingABuiltInPresetMintsAFreshIdentityRatherThanReusingTheBuiltInsUUID`, `testCopyingABetweenMineAndLibraryPreservesIdentity`, `testPreviewImportOfALhpresetFilePreservesItsSourceAndXMPEnvelope`).
- **Verification**: full `swift test` — 1409 executed (up from 1406), 9 skipped (unchanged `RawFixtureTests` fixture-dependent baseline), 0 failures. `git diff --check` — clean. Privacy scan (both changed files individually) — no hits. `swift build` — clean. `xcodebuild -project Apps/LumaHarborPad.xcodeproj -scheme LumaHarborPad -destination 'generic/platform=iOS' CODE_SIGNING_ALLOWED=NO build` — `** BUILD SUCCEEDED **`. `Apps/LumaHarborPad.xcodeproj/project.pbxproj` confirmed untouched before and after.
- **`NOT RUN`**: real-device/Simulator/human-eyes manual check of the fixed Copy/Import flows — same standing limitation as every prior round in this environment.
- **Next action**: Task 3.3 (thumbnail multi-select and batch snapshot semantics). Findings #3/#4 above remain open, non-blocking follow-ups — pick up whenever there's appetite for a Preset UI polish pass, independent of Task 3.3. Do not push, merge, rebase, remove this worktree, or commit any signing setting without explicit user authorization.

## Phase 3 Task 3.2 (2026-09-03, Claude, TDD, in this worktree/branch)

- **Status**: `DONE`. Branch `claude/awayphotoraweditor-parity-phase2-geometry`, product+test commit `6b117b9acd77a586de324b9a4568a759fd15a719`, one commit ahead of Task 3.1's docs commit `41c7095`. Base branch `main` at `fb7109a4fd76035bb9ca3f492b1fa45f511a60ec`, unchanged.

Read directly from `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-02-awayphotoraweditor-parity-roadmap.md` before starting (its detail isn't in this session's own conversation summary, only the task title was). No batch multi-select (Task 3.3), no compound undo (Task 3.4), no virtual copy (Task 3.5).
- **What changed**:
  - `PresetBackupArchive` (new, `PresetCore`) — a portable bundle of every preset in one scope (`schemaVersion`, `createdAt`, `documents: [PresetDocument]`), `.lhpresetbackup`. Distinct from the existing single-preset `.lhpreset`/`.xmp` export. Its own `PresetBackupCoding` (small, deliberate duplication of `PhotoLibraryCore.SidecarCoding`'s conventions, since `PresetCore` doesn't and shouldn't depend on `PhotoLibraryCore` — the dependency runs the other way) rejects a future schema version or malformed JSON via the new `PresetBackupError` (`LocalizedError`, matching `PresetError`'s own convention).
  - `restorePresets(_:into:conflict:)` (new, `PhotoLibraryCore`, alongside the existing `transferPreset`) — replays an archive's documents into a real `PresetRepository`, one `save` per document under the caller's `PresetConflictResolution`, tallied into a new `PresetRestoreSummary` (created/replaced/keptBoth/duplicateSkipped/cancelled/failed) rather than throwing at the first problem — one bad or conflicting document in a large archive must not hide what did restore.
  - `PresetLibraryViewModel.exportBackup(scope:)`/`.restoreBackup(_:into:conflict:)` (new) wire the above into the app; `previewImport(_:)` now also accepts `.lhpreset` files (previously `.xmp` only), via a new cross-module `XMPImportPreview.init` (was internal-only) — an `.lhpreset` import always previews as 100% native fields (no approximate/preserved distinction for LumaHarbor's own format) and always mints a fresh identity, matching the existing `.xmp` import convention (import creates a new preset; it never silently adopts another preset's identity — that's what restore is for, and the two are deliberately kept distinct).
  - `PresetBrowserView` — new "Backup My Presets…" / "Backup This Library's Presets…" (`NSSavePanel`) / "Restore Presets…" (`NSOpenPanel`) menu, next to the existing Import button. Restore always targets "My Presets" under `.keepBoth` (never overwrites, matching `confirmImport`'s own established policy for `.xmp`/`.lhpreset` import) — a **documented scope boundary, not an omission**: a destination-scope or conflict-policy picker is UI-only work for a later round, since `restorePresets`/`PresetRestoreTests` already exercise every policy against a real repository.
  - New localized strings (Backup/Restore menu items and titles, restore-summary vocabulary, error titles/messages) landed in both `en.lproj` and `zh-Hant.lproj`; the pre-existing `.xmp`-only import empty-state copy was reworded to mention `.lhpreset` too.
- **"Preserve unknown XMP fields" (bullet 3)**: mostly a *verification*, not new production code — `XMPEnvelope.originalPacketUTF8` already retained the whole original packet (Phase 1's own XMP round). `PresetBackupArchiveTests.testRoundTripPreservesTheFullOriginalXMPPacketByteForByte` locks this in specifically for the backup/restore path, using a packet with a deliberately unmapped property (`crs:SomeFutureLightroomOnlyProperty2099`), asserting the full packet — not just the mapped fields — survives an archive round trip byte-for-byte.
- **TDD**: RED confirmed at four points, each via a genuine compile failure against the not-yet-existing API (no stash-and-restore needed this round — every new symbol was simply absent, not shadowed by old code). `PresetBackupArchiveTests` (6, new file) against the not-yet-existing `PresetBackupArchive`/`PresetBackupCoding`. `PresetRestoreTests` (7, new file, against the *real* `FilePresetRepository` in a temp directory rather than this codebase's own `RecordingPresetRepository` mock — that mock's `save` always returns `.created` regardless of `conflict` or existing content, so it would never actually exercise a conflict; using the real repository was a deliberate choice, not an oversight). `PresetWorkflowTests`' new 9-test "Backup/restore and .lhpreset import" section against the not-yet-existing `exportBackup`/`restoreBackup`. `PresetBrowserFoundationContractTests` +2 (source-text-contract style) against the not-yet-changed `ImportPresetSheet.swift`/`PresetBrowserView.swift`.
  - **Collateral fix, not scope creep**: `testRestoreBackupAppliesEveryDocumentAndReloadsItems` initially failed (`items.count` 4 instead of 2) for the same reason two Task 3.1 tests needed a fix — `builtInRepository` defaults to the real `BuiltInPresetRepository()` with 2 real presets. Fixed identically: `builtInRepository: BuiltInPresetRepository(documents: [])`.
- **Tests added**: `PresetBackupArchiveTests` (6, new file). `PresetRestoreTests` (7, new file). `PresetWorkflowTests` +9. `PresetBrowserFoundationContractTests` +2. `LocalizationSmokeTest` +1 method (16 keys).

## Phase 3 Task 3.1 (2026-09-03, Claude, TDD, in this worktree/branch)

- **Status**: `DONE`. Branch `claude/awayphotoraweditor-parity-phase2-geometry` (unrenamed — Phase 3 continues in the same worktree/branch the user approved after the Phase 2 P1 fix, per "Phase 2 完成 review 後才開始"), product+test commit `7178bec0734a1900ccb38d22b80a05d73bf11535`, one commit ahead of the Phase 2 follow-ups consolidation `382d799`. Base branch `main` at `fb7109a4fd76035bb9ca3f492b1fa45f511a60ec`, unchanged.
- **Scope**: Phase 3 roadmap Task 3.1 ("Preset — built-in vs. user precedence, sparse patch edit/removal, group/favorite/search, create preset from current photo"). Researched via an Explore subagent before writing any code: group/favorite/search/create-from-photo already existed and were already tested (Phase 1's own preset round), so this task's genuinely new work narrowed to (a) a built-in preset scope and (b) editing an already-saved preset's fields/metadata. Explicitly **not** in scope, per the task's own brief and the roadmap: batch multi-select sync (Task 3.3), virtual copy (Task 3.5).
- **Built-in preset content is a placeholder, by explicit user decision**: asked the user via AskUserQuestion whether to build a full curated preset library (a product/art decision) or a minimal, honestly-labeled technical mechanism proving the scope works end to end; the user chose the latter ("照這個範圍做"). `BuiltInPresetRepository.defaultPresets` ships exactly two presets ("High Contrast", "Flat (Low Contrast)") with fixed UUIDs — not a creative library.
- **What changed**:
  - `BuiltInPresetRepository` (new, `PhotoLibraryCore`) — a third, read-only `PresetRepository` scope, never file-backed (works identically with no Application Support or library volume available). `save`/`delete` always throw the new `PresetError.builtInPresetIsReadOnly` ("Built-in presets can't be changed." / "Duplicate it to My Presets, then edit the copy.").
  - `PresetScopeKind` gained `.builtIn`; `PresetLibraryViewModel` gained a non-optional `builtInRepository` (defaults to the real `BuiltInPresetRepository()`), loads it alongside mine/library in `load()`, and its `ScopeFilter`/`repository(for:)` both handle the new case.
  - `PresetLibraryViewModel.updatePreset(_:name:groupPath:isFavorite:keptFields:)` (new) — edits an already-saved preset in place: same identity, same scope, same `createdAt`. `keptFields` is a subset of whatever the preset's own patch already has; anything currently present but left out is dropped via the existing `AdjustmentPatch.excluding(_:)` sparse-removal primitive (same one the XMP import flow already uses for deselected approximate fields) — this task's "sparse patch edit/removal when a value returns to inherited/default."
  - `EditPresetSheet` (new, `LumaHarborApp`) — mirrors `CreatePresetSheet`'s structure but pre-fills from an existing `PresetListItem`, only shows field-groups that already have at least one present field, and each checkbox can only *remove* a field, never add one that wasn't already there (there's no open photo here to source a new value from). Reuses `CreatePresetSheet`'s `PresetFieldGroup` (made non-`private` for this) rather than a second, drift-prone copy of the same 23-group mapping.
  - `PresetBrowserView`/`PresetRow` — added an "Edit…" menu entry (opens `EditPresetSheet` via a new `editingItem`/`.sheet(item:)`); gated Rename…/Edit…/Delete off entirely for `item.scope == .builtIn` (its only way out is "Copy to My Presets", then edit the copy); added a small source/scope badge next to the preset name — "Built-In" for `.builtIn` items, "Imported" for `.adobeXMP`-sourced ones, absent for the common native-user-preset case — closing the design spec §9.1 gap (no compatibility/source indicator) identified during this task's own research.
  - New localized strings ("Built-In", "Imported", "Edit…", "Edit Preset", "Fields in this preset", the field-removal instructional caption, "Couldn't update this preset") landed in both `en.lproj` and `zh-Hant.lproj`.
- **Known, pre-existing localization gap — not introduced by this task, out of its scope**: the entire `PresetError` `LocalizedError` family (`invalidName`, `readOnlyDestination`, the new `builtInPresetIsReadOnly`, etc.) and some ad-hoc `UserAlert` message bodies distinct from their titles (e.g. `PresetLibraryViewModel`'s "That destination isn't available right now.", used since before this task by `createPreset`/`copy`/`confirmImport` and now also by `updatePreset`) have never had `.strings` entries at all, in either language — confirmed by grep, not assumed. Fixing this systemically is its own, separate round (~40 strings across `PresetError` alone); flagged here for visibility rather than silently left unmentioned or partially/inconsistently patched for just the one new case this task added.
- **TDD**: RED confirmed at three points. `BuiltInPresetRepositoryTests` (7, new file): written and run against the not-yet-existing type, genuine compile failures. `PresetLibraryViewModelTests`'s new built-in-scope (6) and update-preset (4) sections: written and run first, real failures (missing member / wrong item grabbed by `items.first` once built-in items started loading alongside mine — see below). `PresetBrowserFoundationContractTests` (7, new file, source-text-contract style matching `CropOverlayContractTests`/`EyedropperOverlayContractTests`): written before `EditPresetSheet.swift` existed or `PresetBrowserView.swift` was touched; ran RED first (6/7 failing — file-not-found and missing-literal assertions), then implemented the UI pieces to GREEN. `LocalizationSmokeTest.testEveryPresetEditAndBuiltInStringHasAChineseTranslation` (new): RED confirmed (7/7 keys failing, value equalled key) before adding the `.strings` entries.
  - **Collateral fix, not scope creep**: making `builtInRepository` non-optional with a real default broke two *pre-existing* tests (`testLoadMergesMineAndLibraryScopes`, `testScopeFilterNarrowsToOneScopeOrFavorites`) whose item counts/sets were polluted by the two real built-in presets now always loading — fixed by passing `builtInRepository: BuiltInPresetRepository(documents: [])` explicitly in those two tests, the same pattern used for isolating this task's own new tests.
- **Tests added/changed**: `BuiltInPresetRepositoryTests` (7, new file). `PresetWorkflowTests`/`PresetLibraryViewModelTests` +10 (6 built-in scope, 4 update-preset), plus 2 pre-existing tests fixed for the new non-optional default. `PresetBrowserFoundationContractTests` (7, new file). `LocalizationSmokeTest` +1 method (7 keys).
- **Verification**: focused filter (`PresetWorkflowTests|PresetBrowserPresentationTests|PresetDocumentTests|PresetApplicatorTests|BuiltInPresetRepositoryTests|PresetBrowserFoundationContractTests`) — all passing (65 + 7 + 7 = 79 across those suites). Full `swift test` — 1382 executed (up from the Phase 2 P1 fix round's 1357), 9 skipped (unchanged `RawFixtureTests` fixture-dependent baseline), 0 failures. `git diff --check` — clean (exit 0). Privacy scan (`rg -n "/Users/|/Volumes/|/private/|<TEAM_ID>|teamIdentifier:|DEVELOPMENT_TEAM"` over every changed/new file individually) — no hits in any of the 12 files this commit touched. `swift build` — clean. `xcodebuild -project Apps/LumaHarborPad.xcodeproj -scheme LumaHarborPad -destination 'generic/platform=iOS' CODE_SIGNING_ALLOWED=NO build` — `** BUILD SUCCEEDED **` (one pre-existing, unrelated warning: "A launch configuration or launch storyboard or xib must be provided unless the app requires full screen" — present before this task, not introduced by it). `Apps/LumaHarborPad.xcodeproj/project.pbxproj` confirmed untouched (`git status --porcelain` empty for that path) both before and after the iOS build ran.
- **`NOT RUN`**: every real-device/Simulator/human-eyes manual check — this environment has no way to launch and drive the Mac app's UI, so the Edit… menu item, the built-in scope's restricted menu, the source/scope badge's actual on-screen appearance, and `EditPresetSheet`'s field-removal checklist are all only verified via source-text-contract tests, never a human looking at a running window. Consistent with every prior Phase 2 UI round's own `NOT RUN` entry for the same reason.
- **Independent review**: not yet performed for this round.
- **Next action**: Task 3.2 (preset backup/restore and XMP continuation), or hand off for independent review of this round first. Do not push, merge, rebase, remove this worktree, or commit any signing setting without explicit user authorization.

## Phase 2 known follow-ups (P2, non-blocking)

Consolidated from the Task 2.5 verification report's five independent-review focus areas and the P1 fix below, so they live in one place instead of scattered across task-specific entries and commit messages. None of these block Phase 3; each is scoped to its own file(s) and can be picked up independently, in any order, whenever there's appetite for a Phase 2 polish pass.

1. **`CropDragMath` has no handle-swap on crossover** (`Sources/LumaHarborApp/Views/CropDragMath.swift`) — dragging a corner handle past its opposite corner floors at the minimum crop size instead of swapping which handle is which (e.g. dragging `topLeft` past `bottomRight` doesn't turn it into the new `bottomRight`). Covered/pinned by `CropDragMathTests.testDraggingACornerPastItsOppositeCornerFloorsAtTheMinimumSizeRatherThanInverting`, so this is a documented behavior, not a silent gap — just not the polish most crop tools eventually add.
2. **White balance eyedropper direction is self-defined, not verified against `CIRAWFilter`** (`Sources/RawProcessingCore/Model/WhiteBalanceEyedropper.swift`) — `WhiteBalanceEyedropperTests` pins the function's own internally consistent convention (warm cast → cools down, green cast → adds magenta), but nothing in this environment can decode a real RAW file to confirm that convention actually matches what `CIRAWFilter.neutralTemperature`/`.neutralTint` do to real sensor data. Needs a real RAW fixture + manual/human verification before this can be called "correct," not just "self-consistent."
3. **Aspect-ratio picker records intent but doesn't enforce it** (`Sources/AdjustmentUI/GeometryAdjustmentPanel.swift`, `Sources/LumaHarborApp/Views/CropOverlayView.swift`) — choosing Freeform/Original/Square in `GeometryAdjustmentPanel` only sets `PhotoAdjustments.geometry.cropAspectRatio`; `CropDragMath`/`CropOverlayView` never read it, so dragging a handle is always freeform regardless of the picker's selection. The picker is not misleading about *what it does* (it's a straightforward preference field), but a user could reasonably expect it to constrain the drag.
4. **Temperature/tint eyedropper sensitivity constants are a first guess** (`WhiteBalanceEyedropper.temperatureSensitivity`/`.tintSensitivity`) — not calibrated against any reference target or real photo; `ALargerCastProducesALargerMagnitudeDelta` only pins that the *direction* of scaling is monotonic, not that the magnitude feels right in practice.
5. **No continuous hover-before-click eyedropper preview** (`Sources/LumaHarborApp/Views/EyedropperOverlayView.swift`) — the live preview only updates during a press-and-hold drag (`onChanged`/`onEnded`), not on mouse movement before the first click, unlike a typical eyedropper's live-follow-the-cursor feel. Still satisfies the spec's non-committing requirement; this is a UX completeness gap, not a correctness one.

Every real-device/Simulator/human-eyes manual check for crop/rotate/flip/straighten direction and eyedropper cancel/apply (design spec §6.5's own explicit requirement) also remains `NOT RUN` across all of Phase 2 — tracked here for visibility, not repeated as a numbered item since it isn't a code fix, it's a manual verification gap that needs a device.

## Phase 2 independent-review P1 fix (2026-09-03, Claude, TDD, in this worktree/branch)

- **Status**: `DONE`. Fixes the single P1 finding from re-reviewing the whole Phase 2 diff (`fb7109a..HEAD`, i.e. Tasks 2.1–2.5) before allowing Phase 3 to start, per the user's own explicit "Phase 2 完成 review 後才開始" gate. Branch `claude/awayphotoraweditor-parity-phase2-geometry`, product+test commit `0cb185284dfd908cad84373d2d8b247bed017cfb`, one commit ahead of Task 2.5's `5ca6ab1`.
- **Finding**: `GeometryRenderer.apply(_:to:)` (Task 2.2) cropped *before* rotating/flipping/straightening, in the un-rotated source's own normalized coordinates — but `EditorSession.displayedImage`, the exact image `CropOverlayView` (Task 2.3) draws and drags a crop rect against, is always the *fully* rotated/flipped/straightened preview, never the pre-rotation source. This broke two real cases: (1) drawing a brand-new crop after any rotation/flip was already active — the rect dragged on the rotated preview did not correspond to the crop actually applied (wildly wrong for 90/270° rotation, since width/height are swapped between the two coordinate spaces; mirrored for 180° or a flip); (2) re-editing an *already-committed* crop at any rotation — since crop replaces the working image with just that cropped region, `displayedImage` was already the cropped-and-filled result the moment any crop existed, leaving the crop tool no correct frame to show what had been cropped away.
- **Fix**: `GeometryRenderer.apply(_:to:)` now applies crop *last* — after rotate/flip, straighten, and perspective — so it always operates in the same coordinate space `CropOverlayView` shows and drags against, matching how virtually every other photo editor's crop tool behaves. `appliedPixelSize(of:geometry:)` reorders its own prediction to match (rotate-swap first, then crop-scale). `EditorSession.displayedAdjustments` now strips `geometry.crop` specifically while `toolMode == .crop`, so the crop tool's own preview shows the correct pre-crop (but post-rotate/flip/straighten/perspective) reference frame for both a first crop and adjusting an existing one — the *committed* crop (`editor.adjustments.geometry.crop`) is untouched; only what's rendered while the tool is open changes. `PhotoExporter`/`CoreImagePreviewRenderer` needed no changes: both already call the same `GeometryRenderer.apply(_:to:)`, and export always uses the real committed adjustments (never `displayedAdjustments`), so this fix cannot affect what an export actually contains.
- **TDD**: RED confirmed by first rewriting `GeometryRendererTests`' crop+rotate combined test and `appliedPixelSize`'s combined test to assert the *new* (correct) expected behavior, running them against the pre-fix code, and getting genuine failures (e.g. extent 4×8 instead of the expected 8×4; predicted size (1000,1000) instead of (500,2000) for an asymmetric crop fraction deliberately chosen to distinguish the two orders — a symmetric 0.5×0.5 crop produces the same numbers either way and would have hidden the bug). Same for `EditorSessionEditingTests`' three new `displayedAdjustments` tests, written and run against the pre-fix `EditorSession` first.
- **Tests changed**: `GeometryRendererTests` — renamed/rewrote `testCropThenRotateAppliesCropInTheSourcesOwnCoordinateSpace` → `testRotateThenCropAppliesCropInTheAlreadyRotatedCoordinateSpace` and `testAppliedPixelSizeCombinesCropAndRotate` → `testAppliedPixelSizeCombinesRotateAndCrop`. `EditorSessionEditingTests` +3. `PhotoExportTests`: no assertion changes needed (both existing crop+rotate export tests happen to use either a symmetric crop fraction or no rotation, so neither distinguishes old vs. new order), only a corrected explanatory comment.
- **Verification**: full `swift test` — 1357 executed (up from Task 2.5's 1354; net +3 from the new `EditorSession` tests), 9 skipped (unchanged `RawFixtureTests` fixture-dependent baseline), 0 failures. `git diff --check` — clean. Privacy scan — no hits. `swift build` — clean. `xcodebuild -project Apps/LumaHarborPad.xcodeproj -scheme LumaHarborPad -destination 'generic/platform=iOS' -derivedDataPath /private/tmp/LumaHarbor-Phase2P1Fix-DerivedData CODE_SIGNING_ALLOWED=NO build` — `** BUILD SUCCEEDED **`. No `Apps/` file touched; `project.pbxproj` remained untouched throughout.
- **Explicitly not fixed in this round, still open for a future pass (unaffected by this fix)**: `CropDragMath`'s past-opposite-corner floor behavior, the eyedropper's self-defined (unverified against `CIRAWFilter`) direction convention, and the aspect-ratio picker's not-yet-enforced intent — the other three items already named in the Task 2.5 report's independent-review focus areas.
- **Next action**: this was a fix-only round covering one of the five independent-review focus areas from the Task 2.5 report. Recommend either a quick re-review of this specific fix, or proceeding to Phase 3 (Preset, Batch, Virtual Copy) per the user's own call, with the remaining four focus areas tracked as known follow-ups rather than blockers. Do not push, merge, rebase, remove this worktree, or commit any signing setting without explicit user authorization.

## Phase 2 Task 2.4 (2026-09-03, Claude, TDD, in this worktree/branch)

- **Status**: `DONE`. Branch `claude/awayphotoraweditor-parity-phase2-geometry`, product commit `3b8bbcfbf64d46702d8477be9438f936a9c697a0`, one commit ahead of Task 2.3's `a1c178f` (this file's own docs commit). Base branch `main` at `fb7109a4fd76035bb9ca3f492b1fa45f511a60ec`, unchanged.
- **Scope**: Phase 2 roadmap Task 2.4 ("White balance eyedropper"). Task 2.5 (Phase 2 verification/independent review) remains separate, not started.
- **What changed**:
  - `WhiteBalanceEyedropper` (new, `RawProcessingCore`) — a pure, self-contained RGB-to-slider-delta estimate. Deliberately does **not** call `CIRAWFilter`'s own `neutralLocation`/`neutralTemperature` API (confirmed unused anywhere in this codebase before starting — its algorithm is proprietary and there is no real RAW fixture in this environment to verify against). Works from already-decoded/rendered RGB instead, matching design spec §6.4's own "一般影像格式則以已解碼 RGB 估算" framing; the RAW-specific "as-shot metadata baseline" half of §6.4 falls out for free since `EditorSession` applies the delta on top of the already-committed temperature/tint, which is itself already an offset from `RawWhiteBalanceBaseline`. Direction is self-consistently defined and pinned by tests (warm cast → cools down, matching `AdjustmentMapping`'s own documented "+temperature warms" convention; green cast → adds magenta) — an honest "first version, estimate," not a claim of matching `CIRAWFilter`'s exact physics.
  - `PixelSampler` (new, `RawProcessingCore`) — reads one pixel's colour out of an already-rendered `CGImage`, promoting this codebase's own existing test-only technique (`AdjustmentPipelineTests.pixel(at:in:)`'s `CGContext`-flip trick) to production code, with the same top-left-origin convention `NormalizedCropRect`/`AspectFitRect` already use.
  - `AspectFitRect.imagePixel(at:imageFrame:imageSize:)` (new) — the inverse of `fitting(imageSize:in:padding:)`: maps a click location back to an image-pixel coordinate, clamped into bounds.
  - `EditorSession` gained `previewEyedropper(sample:)`/`cancelEyedropperPreview()`/`commitEyedropper()`, extending the exact non-committing preview pattern `previewPreset(_:mode:)`/`cancelPresetPreview()`/`commitPreset(_:mode:)` already established for presets (`displayedAdjustments` now checks the eyedropper preview slot first, then the preset one, then `history.current`) — a **separate** field (`previewedEyedropperAdjustments`) rather than reusing the preset one, so an eyedropper drag and a preset-browser hover (nothing currently prevents both being live at once) can never clobber each other. Reuses `previewIntentVersion`/`previewImageReflectsAPreview` for the same stale-frame protection presets already have. `EditorToolMode` gained `.whiteBalance` (its own doc comment from Task 2.3 already earmarked this extension point).
  - `EyedropperOverlayView` (new, `LumaHarborApp`) — the on-canvas hit area, mounted in `EditorView.previewArea` only when `toolMode == .whiteBalance`, built from the same `AspectFitRect.fitting(...)` frame `CropOverlayView` (Task 2.3) uses. A press previews live on every `onChanged`; release commits and returns to `.adjust`. `WhiteBalanceEyedropperButton` (new) toggles the tool from next to the "Color" group header (`ColorAdjustmentPanel`'s own doc comment already notes temperature/tint live in `BasicAdjustmentPanel`) — turning it off while a sample is live explicitly calls `cancelEyedropperPreview()` first, satisfying the spec's own cancel requirement (§6.4: "使用者必須能取消滴管") rather than leaving it to be silently dropped.
  - New localized strings: `"White Balance Eyedropper"`, `"Cancel Eyedropper"`, `"Click a point that should be neutral gray"` — landed in both `en.lproj` and `zh-Hant.lproj`.
- **TDD**: RED confirmed at three points via this repo's established stash-and-restore pattern. `WhiteBalanceEyedropper`/`PixelSampler`/`AspectFitRect.imagePixel`: written and tested before any `EditorSession`/UI wiring existed, each against real Core Graphics-space math, passing on first run. `EditorSession`'s three new methods: tests written first (mirroring `PresetWorkflowTests`' own assertion style), genuine "has no member" compile failures against the not-yet-existing API, then implemented and passed on first run. Whole-round wiring: stashed every product file (SHA `ee5aceb91df2916c3afb8923930b9f4dfa4489c3`) with every new test already in place and got compile failures across multiple test files at once; restored via `git stash apply` (not `pop`) and dropped afterward.
- **Tests added**: `WhiteBalanceEyedropperTests` (11, new file). `PixelSamplerTests` (6, new file). `AspectFitRectTests` +5 (`imagePixel` corners/centre/non-square/out-of-bounds/zero-frame). `EditorSessionEditingTests` +12 (preview/cancel/commit contract, no history entry for a neutral sample, second sample replaces rather than compounds, no-op without an open photo, `open()`/`close()` both clear a live preview). `EyedropperOverlayContractTests` (6, new file). `LocalizationSmokeTest` +1.
- **Verification**: focused filter covering every file touched — all passing. Full `swift test` — 1354 executed (up from Task 2.3's 1313), 9 skipped (unchanged `RawFixtureTests` fixture-dependent baseline), 0 failures. `git diff --check` — clean. Privacy scan (`rg -n "/Users/|/Volumes/|/private/|<TEAM_ID>|teamIdentifier:|DEVELOPMENT_TEAM"` over the diff and every new untracked file) — no hits. `swift build` — clean. `xcodebuild -project Apps/LumaHarborPad.xcodeproj -scheme LumaHarborPad -destination 'generic/platform=iOS' -derivedDataPath /private/tmp/LumaHarbor-Phase2Task2_4-DerivedData CODE_SIGNING_ALLOWED=NO build` — `** BUILD SUCCEEDED **`. No `Apps/` file was touched; `git status` confirmed `Apps/LumaHarborPad.xcodeproj/project.pbxproj` stayed clean throughout, both before and after the iOS build ran.
- **`NOT RUN`**: every real-device/Simulator/human-eyes check — whether the eyedropper's warm/cool and green/magenta direction actually *looks* right on a real photo, whether the ring marker and click hit-target feel right, and any interactive drag verification in a live running app. This environment has no way to launch and drive the Mac app's UI. The direction this round pins is the function's own self-consistent, documented convention (verified by unit test), not a human confirming it matches intuition on an actual RAW photo — consistent with how `GeometryRenderer`'s rotate direction (Task 2.2) and `CropDragMath`'s handle directions (Task 2.3) were each pinned the same way, and each still awaits that same eventual manual check.
- **Known, documented scope boundaries, not omissions**: no continuous hover-before-click preview (only live-updates during a press-and-hold drag, which still satisfies the non-committing requirement); no region/small-area averaging (spec allows "a point or a small region", this samples exactly one pixel); the temperature/tint sensitivity constants are a first-guess, not calibrated against any reference.
- **Not started**: Task 2.5 (Phase 2 verification/independent review).
- **Independent review**: not yet performed for this round, or for Tasks 2.1–2.3.
- **Next action**: Task 2.5 (Phase 2 verification: full test/build gates already largely covered per-task above, plus the manual screenshot checklist and a testing report under `docs/testing/reports/`), or hand off for independent review of Tasks 2.1–2.4 together first. ## Phase 2 Task 2.3 (2026-09-03, Claude, TDD, in this worktree/branch)

- **Status**: `DONE`. Branch `claude/awayphotoraweditor-parity-phase2-geometry`, product commit `9e93d72cb4b34a6167597fd43a52bdfd2e23f9b1`, one commit ahead of Task 2.2's `c9e8383` (this file's own docs commit). Base branch `main` at `fb7109a4fd76035bb9ca3f492b1fa45f511a60ec`, unchanged.
- **Scope**: Phase 2 roadmap Task 2.3 ("Mac crop/rotate/straighten UI"), continued directly from Task 2.2 at the user's own "接續" instruction (no separate review checkpoint requested for this round). No white balance eyedropper (Task 2.4), no Phase 2 verification/independent review pass (Task 2.5) — those remain separate, not-started tasks.
- **What changed**: this is the interaction layer only — Task 2.1's model and Task 2.2's render pipeline already existed and already flowed into the preview; this task adds the first gesture recognizer ever placed on the photo canvas in this app.
  - `EditorToolMode` (new, `EditorCore`) — the first "what on-canvas tool is active" concept in this codebase (previously only per-feature booleans like `isShowingOriginal` existed). `EditorSession.toolMode` (`.adjust`/`.crop`) resets to `.adjust` on both `open()` and `close()`, so switching photos never leaves a crop overlay armed against a photo nobody asked to crop. `setToolMode(_:)` is deliberately pure UI state — it never touches `history`, `saveState`, or the preview, unlike every other method in `EditorSession`'s editing section.
  - `GeometryAdjustmentPanel` (new, `AdjustmentUI`) — `InspectorView`'s sixth group (design spec has no "Geometry" among §6.3's Basic/Color/Curve/Detail/Effects; §6.5 describes geometry as its own feature area). Rotate/flip/straighten route through `updateAdjustments(_:)` using `GeometryAdjustments`'s own `rotatedClockwise()`/`flippingHorizontal()`/`resettingStraighten()` etc. helpers from Task 2.1. "Edit Crop" calls `editor.setToolMode(.crop)` instead of editing a value directly — the crop rect itself is a canvas gesture, not a slider. The aspect ratio picker (Freeform/Original/Square) records the lock *preference* only; it does not yet enforce it during a drag or auto-apply a centered rect on selection — a documented scope boundary, not an oversight (see the commit message for why: the only aspect ratio available from inside this panel today is the already-displayed, possibly-already-cropped preview's own, which is the wrong reference once a crop is active).
  - `CropOverlayView` (new, `LumaHarborApp`) — draws inside `EditorView.previewArea` only when `toolMode == .crop`: a dimmed scrim (even-odd `Path` fill, outer rect minus the crop rect), a white border, four corner-handle drag targets, and an interior move-drag target. All routed through `EditorSession.updateAdjustments(_:)`, so undo/autosave/preview-refresh come free with no separate wiring. Landing a drag back on the exact full frame writes `crop = nil` rather than a redundant `.full` value, keeping `GeometryAdjustments.isIdentity`/`EditorSession.hasEdits` exact.
  - `CropDragMath` (new, pure, `LumaHarborApp`) — every drag's direction/fixed-corner math, unit-tested independently of any gesture ever firing, following the same "state the expected fixed corner explicitly, don't trust the formula by inspection" discipline `GeometryRenderer`'s rotate-direction tests established in Task 2.2.
  - `AspectFitRect` (new, pure, `LumaHarborApp`) — reproduces `EditorView.previewArea`'s own `Image(...).resizable().aspectRatio(contentMode: .fit).padding(16)` layout as a testable `CGRect` calculation, so the overlay lines up with the exact rectangle the photo occupies. Both SwiftUI's own coordinate space and `NormalizedCropRect`'s (top-left origin, y-down) agree, so no axis flip is needed here — that only happens once, inside `GeometryRenderer`, converting into Core Image's y-up native space.
  - Localization: new key `"Geometry adjustments are non-destructive."` alongside the export sheet's existing `"Your RAW original was not changed."` (reused, not duplicated) — both required by the roadmap's own "Add localized safety copy: geometry is non-destructive and RAW original is unchanged." `"Reset"` and `"Original"` are likewise reused from existing keys. 14 new keys landed in both `en.lproj` and `zh-Hant.lproj`.
- **TDD**: this codebase has no runtime SwiftUI view-inspection dependency (`Package.swift` has zero third-party dependencies, confirmed before starting) — every UI-shape assertion follows the established source-text-contract pattern (`String(contentsOf:)` the `.swift` file, assert it contains specific literals) the same way `InspectorAdjustmentGroupsContractTests`/`AdjustmentGroupPanelsContractTests` already do. RED confirmed for the whole round at once via this repo's stash-and-restore pattern: stashed every product file (`EditorSession.swift`, `EditorToolMode.swift`, `EditorView.swift`, `InspectorView.swift`, `AspectFitRect.swift`, `CropDragMath.swift`, `CropOverlayView.swift`, `GeometryAdjustmentPanel.swift`, both `Localizable.strings`) back to pre-Task-2.3 content (SHA `08cb2328b2b98521be7b3cd9114142e1528dc4ce`) with every new test already in place, and got genuine compile failures (`value of type 'EditorSession' has no member 'toolMode'`, etc.), not just failing assertions. Restored via `git stash apply` (not `pop`) and dropped afterward; `git stash list` was empty before continuing. The two pure-function pieces (`AspectFitRect`, `CropDragMath`) were each worked out by hand against real Core Graphics-space math and passed on first run — their tests still assert the fixed-corner/centered-fit properties explicitly rather than only checking "compiles and doesn't crash".
- **Tests added**: `EditorSessionEditingTests` +5 (tool mode default, switching, no undo/save-state side effect, reset on `open()` and on `close()`). `AspectFitRectTests` (6, new file). `CropDragMathTests` (8, new file: move translate-only and edge-clamped, all four corner handles' fixed-corner behavior stated explicitly, past-opposite-corner floor behavior, zero-frame-size no-op). `AdjustmentGroupPanelsContractTests` +1 (Geometry panel coverage). `InspectorAdjustmentGroupsContractTests` extended to cover the sixth Geometry group. `CropOverlayContractTests` (4, new file). `LocalizationSmokeTest` +1 (all 15 new geometry-tool keys have a Traditional Chinese translation).

## Phase 2 Task 2 (2026-09-03, Claude, TDD, in this worktree/branch)

- **Status**: `DONE`. Branch `claude/awayphotoraweditor-parity-phase2-geometry`, product commit `36fb7b7f1768781b17d78234d168fbc02c6fdd1d`, one commit ahead of Task 1's `77bf2cd` (this file's own docs commit). Base branch `main` at `fb7109a4fd76035bb9ca3f492b1fa45f511a60ec`, unchanged.
- **Scope**: Phase 2 roadmap Task 2.2 only ("Geometry render pipeline"), approved by the user to start after Task 2.1. Wires the model from Task 1 into actual rendering — no Mac UI (Task 2.3), no white balance eyedropper (Task 2.4).
- **What changed**: `Sources/RawProcessingCore/Pipeline/GeometryRenderer.swift` (new) applies `GeometryAdjustments` to a `CIImage` in the roadmap's documented order — crop (source's own pre-rotate normalized coordinates) → rotate 90°/flip → straighten → perspective — leaving resize/export to the caller (`ExportResizing`). Rotation/straighten degrees are documented and implemented as *clockwise* (matching `GeometryAdjustments.rotatedClockwise()`), the opposite sign of `CGAffineTransform(rotationAngle:)`'s native counterclockwise-for-positive convention in Core Image's y-up space — negated in `clockwiseRadians(_:)`, pinned by a quadrant-marker-image test. Straighten and perspective are clamped back to their input canvas (`.transformed(by:).cropped(to: extent)`) rather than letting the extent grow to the rotated bounding box, trading auto-fit for an exactly predictable output size — the property `appliedPixelSize(of:geometry:)` depends on to report an export's size from a cheap metadata read alone, without a second full decode.
- **Both preview and export now share this code path**: `CoreImagePreviewRenderer.render(_:)` calls `GeometryRenderer.apply(request.adjustments.geometry, to: adjusted)` before `makeCGImage(_:)` — `PreviewImage.pixelSize` already derives from the rendered `CGImage`'s own width/height, so this one line is the entire "crop preview integration" requirement. `PhotoExporter.renderToDisk` calls the same function before its own resize step.
- **Bug caught and fixed in the same round, not a separate task**: the export's resize-fitting transform previously fit `maximumWidth`/`maximumHeight` against `decoded.nativePixelSize` (the *uncropped* native decode size). Now it fits against `withGeometry.extent.size` (the real, already-geometry-adjusted extent) instead. Caught by a RED test (`testMaximumWidthFitsAgainstTheCroppedSizeNotTheNativeSize`) using a crop whose aspect ratio differs from its source, which distinguishes "fit against the wrong reference" (500×250, the bug) from "fit against the actual cropped image" (500×500, correct) — a same-aspect-ratio crop would have hidden this bug by coincidence, which is why the first draft of this test (numbers chosen without checking) passed even against the un-wired code and had to be redesigned. `ExportOutcome.pixelSize`'s own separate metadata-only size report (computed in a second stage that deliberately runs after the temp file is already on disk, load-bearing for an existing cancellation-timing test with a `metadataGate`) now runs through `GeometryRenderer.appliedPixelSize(of:geometry:)` instead of raw `ExportResizing.fittedSize(nativeSize: nativePixelSize, ...)`.
- **TDD**: RED confirmed at two levels via this repo's established stash-and-restore pattern (`git stash push -u -m`, `apply` not `pop`, then `drop`). `GeometryRenderer` itself: no product file existed yet, so the first run was a genuine compile failure. The rotate-90/270 direction and the straighten-movement assertion each needed one real empirical iteration against actual Core Image render output before landing on the correct sign (`clockwiseRadians`) and a sample point with enough radius from the rotation centre to be non-flaky (both are now documented in the code, not just discovered and silently discarded — see `GeometryRenderer.clockwiseRadians(_:)`'s own doc comment for the direction finding). Preview/export wiring: stashed `PhotoExporter.swift` and `CoreImagePreviewRenderer.swift` back to their pre-Task-2.2 content (SHA `3fb5f27b65e9da7661687bec12b204995aa3b7d0`) with `GeometryRenderer.swift` itself still present, then the five new integration tests failed with real dimension-mismatch assertions (e.g. `(4000.0, 3000.0)` instead of the expected `(2000.0, 1500.0)` for a half-crop) — a stronger RED than a compile error, proving the *wiring* is load-bearing, not just the type's existence. Restored via `git stash apply` and dropped afterward; `git stash list` was empty before continuing both times.
- **Tests added**: `Tests/RawProcessingCoreTests/GeometryRendererTests.swift` (new file, 25 tests: neutral identity exact passthrough, rotate 90/180/270 exact quadrant-corner colour assertions on a four-quadrant marker image, rotate-90 dimension swap, flip horizontal/vertical exact, flip-both-equals-rotate-180, crop exact to a quarter and combined with rotate in the documented order, straighten and perspective as tolerance/property-based checks per the roadmap's own "not pixel-perfect for interpolated transforms" guidance, `appliedPixelSize`'s pure-function cases including the crop+rotate combination). `PhotoExportTests` +5 (crop/rotate/combined output-dimension assertions checked against the actual written file via `CGImageSourceCreateImageAtIndex`, the maximumWidth-fits-against-cropped-size regression test, an explicit neutral-geometry baseline). `CoreImagePreviewRendererTests` +2 (crop reflected in both `PreviewImage.pixelSize` and the actual `CGImage`'s own dimensions; a neutral-geometry baseline).
- **Verification**: focused filter (`GeometryRendererTests|PhotoExportTests|CoreImagePreviewRendererTests|AdjustmentPipelineTests|AdjustmentMappingTests|GeometryAdjustmentsTests|NormalizedCropRectTests|CropAspectRatioTests|PhotoAdjustmentsTests`) — 174 executed, 0 failures. Full `swift test` — 1288 executed (up from Task 1's 1256), 9 skipped (unchanged `RawFixtureTests` fixture-dependent baseline), 0 failures. `git diff --check` — clean. Privacy scan (`rg -n "/Users/|/Volumes/|/private/|<TEAM_ID>|teamIdentifier:|DEVELOPMENT_TEAM"` over the diff and both new untracked files) — no hits. `swift build` — clean. `xcodebuild -project Apps/LumaHarborPad.xcodeproj -scheme LumaHarborPad -destination 'generic/platform=iOS' -derivedDataPath /private/tmp/LumaHarbor-Phase2Task2-DerivedData CODE_SIGNING_ALLOWED=NO build` — `** BUILD SUCCEEDED **`, confirming the shared `RawProcessingCore` change doesn't break the iPad app. No iPad app file was touched; `git status` confirmed `Apps/LumaHarborPad.xcodeproj/project.pbxproj` stayed clean throughout, both before and after the iOS build ran.
- **`NOT RUN`**: every real-device/Simulator/manual check. This round has no UI of its own to check by hand — Task 2.3 (Mac crop/rotate/straighten UI) is where a manual crop-direction/rotate-direction screenshot checklist (the roadmap's own §6.5 requirement: "裁切與旋轉的 UI 正負方向必須用真人或 screenshot fixture 驗證，不能只用數學座標直覺決定") becomes directly relevant — this round's rotate/flip direction was pinned by synthetic-image tests, not by a human looking at a screen, which is a real but partial substitute the roadmap itself anticipates ("Use tolerance-based image assertions") without waiving the eventual manual check.
- **Known, documented limitation, not a bug**: straighten/perspective revealing transparent (renders as black, since the test images have no alpha handling downstream) corners within the working canvas rather than auto-cropping or growing the canvas. Intentional — see `GeometryRenderer.straightened(_:degrees:)`'s doc comment — but worth a second pair of eyes before Task 2.3 commits to a specific crop-after-straighten UI flow around it.

- **Scope**: Phase 2 roadmap Task 2.1 only (`docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-02-awayphotoraweditor-parity-roadmap.md`, "Geometry model and sidecar compatibility"). Model + sidecar compatibility only — no render pipeline wiring, no Mac UI, no white balance eyedropper.
- **What changed**: `Sources/RawProcessingCore/Model/GeometryAdjustments.swift` (new) adds `GeometryAdjustments` (crop/rotation/flip/straighten/perspective), `NormalizedCropRect` (a `[0,1]`-normalized, self-validating crop rectangle), and `CropAspectRatio` (freeform/original/square/custom, with a `fixedRatio` pure helper). `PhotoAdjustments` gained a `geometry: GeometryAdjustments` field using the exact same backward-compatible Codable convention every other sub-adjustment (`vignette`, `grain`, etc.) already uses: `decodeIfPresent(...) ?? .neutral`, so a sidecar written before this field existed decodes to neutral geometry rather than failing to open. Rotation snaps to the nearest 90° and wraps into `[0, 360)` (§6.5: "旋轉 90 度" is a discrete action, not a free slider); straighten clamps to ±45; perspective clamps to ±100 (percentage-style span, matching the other ±100 sliders); `NormalizedCropRect` keeps a requested crop's size and slides its origin back into `[0,1]` rather than shrinking it when it would run past an edge, and floors width/height at a minimum (`0.01`) instead of allowing a zero-area crop. Every non-finite input (NaN/Infinity) falls back to a safe default (0, or the full frame for a crop rect) instead of propagating.
- **Render pipeline explicitly untouched**: `AdjustmentMapping.renderParameters(for:)` / `RenderParameters` do not read `geometry` at all — that wiring is Task 2.2's job. `AdjustmentMappingTests.testGeometryNeverAffectsRenderParameters` and `testNeutralGeometryDoesNotChangeTheNeutralIdentityRenderParameters` pin that two `PhotoAdjustments` differing only in `geometry` (neutral or heavily edited) render identically today, so adding this field cannot change any existing photo's rendered output — the round's own concrete evidence for "neutral geometry 不會改變既有 render 行為," beyond the trivial fact that no existing call site reads the new field.
- **TDD**: RED confirmed via this repo's established stash-and-restore pattern (same technique as Phase 1 Task 4's evidence entry below). `git stash push -u -m "phase2-task1-red-check-geometry-product" -- Sources/RawProcessingCore/Model/PhotoAdjustments.swift Sources/RawProcessingCore/Model/GeometryAdjustments.swift` (SHA `ed55fa956019b4425d7e7d2d99dbe24b9b586eff`), then the new tests were run against the reverted product tree and failed with genuine compile errors (`cannot find 'GeometryAdjustments' in scope`, `value of type 'PhotoAdjustments' has no member 'geometry'`), not just failing assertions. Restored via `git stash apply` (not `pop`) and dropped the entry afterward; `git stash list` was empty again before continuing. One test bug was caught and fixed during the GREEN pass itself (not a product bug): the first draft of `testEncodesEveryDocumentedKey` mistakenly listed `"crop"` in the expected key set for a *neutral* encode, when `crop` is `nil` for neutral and `encodeIfPresent` correctly omits the key — split into `testEncodesEveryDocumentedKeyWhenCropIsSet` (crop present) and `testNeutralOmitsTheCropKeyEntirelyRatherThanEncodingNull` (crop absent, asserted explicitly).
