## Phase 1 independent-review P1 fix (2026-09-02, Claude, TDD, in this worktree/branch)

- **Status**: `DONE`. Fixes the single P1 finding from the independent review of `8a400ed..ed40675` (Task 5's own report/CURRENT.md entries above are otherwise unchanged and still describe that state accurately). Branch `codex/awayphotoraweditor-parity-phase1`.
- **Finding**: `ExportMetadataBuilder.imageProperties(from:)` wrote `metadata.orientation` (the *source RAW file's own* un-rotated EXIF tag, read straight off disk by `CoreImageRawDecoder.readMetadata`/`decode`, independent of `CIRAWFilter`) into the exported file's properties. But the pixels those properties get attached to are `CIRAWFilter.outputImage`'s output, which is already rotated to display orientation (corroborated by this codebase's own pre-existing `RawFixtureTests.testFullResolutionExportMatchesTheSourceDimensions` comment: "Orientation may swap the axes, so compare the pair rather than each side"). Writing the source's raw tag onto already-corrected pixels would tell any EXIF-aware viewer (Preview, Photos, Finder, browsers) to rotate an already-upright image a second time — affecting the *default* `exifRetentionPolicy: .preserveAll` and also `.partial`, for any photo shot with a non-normal orientation (portrait, rotated).
- **Fix**: `ExportMetadataBuilder.imageProperties(from:)` no longer writes `kCGImagePropertyOrientation` at all, for any value. Omitting it is the semantically correct "no further rotation needed" state for already-oriented pixels; no special-casing "value happens to be 1" keeps the rule simple. `ExifRetentionPolicy.removeAll` already avoided this bug by dropping all metadata (including orientation) and is unaffected. Only file touched in `Sources/`: `Sources/RawProcessingCore/Export/ExportMetadataBuilder.swift`.
- **TDD**: RED confirmed at two levels before the fix. Pure-function level: replaced the old `ExportMetadataBuilderTests.testOrientationLandsAtTheTopLevel` (which asserted the *buggy* behavior — that orientation 6 lands in the properties dict unchanged) with `testOrientationIsNeverWrittenSincePixelsAreAlreadyOrientedBeforeThisRuns`, which failed for every non-nil orientation value (1, 3, 6, 8) against the pre-fix builder. End-to-end level: added `PhotoExportTests.testPreserveAllNeverWritesTheSourcesOrientationTagIntoTheExportedFile` and `testPartialNeverWritesTheSourcesOrientationTagIntoTheExportedFile` (both real export → `CGImageSourceCopyPropertiesAtIndex` round-trip via a `SyntheticRawDecoder` with `metadataOverride: RawMetadata(orientation: 6)`), which failed with the source's literal `6` leaking into the exported TIFF's properties. A third test, `testRemoveAllStillNeverWritesOrientation`, was added as a regression guard and was already green before the fix (confirming `.removeAll` never had this problem). The end-to-end assertions accept "absent or `1` (normal)" rather than strictly "absent," since TIFF's own encoder was observed (by running the test) to stamp a default orientation of `1` even when the caller writes nothing — semantically correct either way, unlike the source's actual `6`.
- **Verification**: focused — `ExportMetadataBuilderTests` 5 executed/0 failures, `PhotoExportTests` 34 executed/0 failures (69 total across all Task 4 suites: `ExportFormatTests`+`ExportResizingTests`+`ExifRetentionPolicyTests`+`ExportMetadataBuilderTests`+`PhotoExportTests`+`ExportSheetContractTests`). Full `swift test`: 1215 executed (up from 1212), 9 skipped (unchanged `RawFixtureTests` fixture-dependent baseline), 0 failures. `git diff --check`: clean. Privacy scan (`rg -n "/Users/|/Volumes/|/private/|<TEAM_ID>|teamIdentifier:|DEVELOPMENT_TEAM"` over the diff): no hits. Changed exactly the 3 files listed above (1 product file, 2 test files) — no signing/project-local setting touched.
- **Explicitly out of scope for this round, per the user's own instruction**: every P2/P3 finding from the independent review (the "byte-for-byte equivalent" wording, missing per-format sRGB-tag test coverage, the missing "色彩描述" metadata field, unclamped DPI input, `ExifRetentionPolicy.partial`'s field-selection judgment call, the `AdjustmentSliderRow` Sendable-closure warning) — none of those were touched.
- **Next action**: this was a fix-only round; Phase 2 has not started. Recommend either a quick re-review of this specific fix, or proceeding to close out Phase 1 / start Phase 2 per the user's own call. Do not push, merge, rebase, remove this worktree, or commit any signing setting without explicit user authorization.

## Phase 1 Task 4 (2026-09-02, Claude, TDD, in this worktree/branch)

- **Status**: `DONE`. Branch `codex/awayphotoraweditor-parity-phase1`, HEAD `f0dd922abf5770f88f6096e25760c030dde1a779`, one commit ahead of `9c3cb15` (Task 3's own docs commit). Base branch `main` at `8a400edb0f07082d157abb28b9c688d18db98f34`.
- **What changed**: `Sources/RawProcessingCore/Export/JPEGExporter.swift` was renamed to `PhotoExporter.swift` and generalized behind a format-aware `ExportRequest` (JPEG/PNG/TIFF/HEIC), while keeping default (`format: .jpeg`, `quality: 0.9`) output byte-for-byte equivalent to the old JPEG-only behavior — every pre-existing JPEG test (renamed `PhotoExportTests.swift`, formerly `JPEGExportTests.swift`) still passes unchanged. Four new pure, offline-testable types were added to `Sources/RawProcessingCore/Export/`: `ExportFormat` (extension/UTI/quality-and-bit-depth-applicability/`isSupported(by:)` capability detection against an injectable encodable-UTI set), `ExportBitDepth` (TIFF-only 8/16-bit), `ExifRetentionPolicy` (preserveAll/removeAll/partial — a pure `RawMetadata -> RawMetadata` transform; `partial` strips capture date and camera/lens identity while keeping exposure technical fields, a documented reading of "partial" since `RawMetadata` has no GPS field to strip), and `ExportResizing` (aspect-preserving max-width/max-height fitting, never upscales). `ExportMetadataBuilder` turns a (possibly policy-filtered) `RawMetadata` into an ImageIO properties dictionary, mirroring the existing `RawMetadata.from(imageProperties:)` decode-side parser in reverse.
- **Capability guard**: `PhotoExporter.export(_:)` checks `ExportFormat.isSupported(by:)` before touching disk and throws `ExportError.formatNotSupported` instead of pretending success on a build with no encoder for the requested format (plan: "if a platform cannot encode one format, show disabled/unsupported UI instead of pretending success"). The Mac `ExportSheet` marks unsupported formats "(Not Supported)" in the picker and disables the export action outright when the selected format has no encoder.
- **Full-resolution, not preview cache**: every `RawDecodeRequest` the exporter submits uses `quality: .full`, confirmed directly by a new recording-decoder test (`testExportAlwaysRequestsAFullQualityDecodeRegardlessOfPreviewState`), not just inferred from pixel-size assertions.
- **Render-path finding (mid-round correction)**: the first implementation tried to pass DPI and EXIF/TIFF metadata dictionaries into `CIContext`'s `...Representation` convenience methods via `CIImageRepresentationOption`, the same mechanism the pre-existing `writeJPEG(_:to:quality:)` uses for compression quality. Real round-trip tests (write a file, read `CGImageSourceCopyPropertiesAtIndex` back) showed this silently drops arbitrary ImageIO property keys — only a small documented allowlist (compression quality among them) is actually honored. `ImageRenderService.writeExport(...)` now goes through `CGImageDestination` directly (`CGImageDestinationAddImage`'s own properties dictionary, the documented general mechanism for this), which the same round-trip tests confirm actually carries DPI, TIFF/EXIF dictionaries, and per-format bit depth into the file. `writeJPEG`/`jpegData` (used by the preview renderer and thumbnail provider) were not touched.
- **Mac UI**: `ExportSheet` gained format/quality/bit-depth/max-width/max-height/DPI/EXIF-retention controls, bundled into a new `MacExportOptions` (`LibraryViewModel.swift`) that maps 1:1 onto `ExportRequest`'s new fields. Quality only shows for formats that use it; bit depth only for TIFF. The RAW-safety copy ("re-decodes the original RAW at full resolution...", "Your RAW original was not changed.") and the existing Exported/Exporting/Cancel per-export state block are preserved. The toolbar's Export button tooltip and the NSOpenPanel title were generalized from JPEG-specific wording ("Export a full-resolution JPEG" / "Export JPEG") to format-neutral copy; the old, now-unreferenced localization keys were left in place rather than deleted, matching this branch's established Task 1/2 precedent for additive-only localization work.
- **TDD**: RED confirmed for the model/exporter layer via genuine compile failures (`cannot find 'ExportFormat' in scope`, etc.) against the not-yet-existing types. RED confirmed for the UI layer using this repo's own established stash-and-restore pattern (see the Task 2 evidence entry below for precedent): `git stash push -u -m "task4-red-check-exportsheet-librarymodel" -- Sources/LumaHarborApp/Views/ExportSheet.swift Sources/LumaHarborApp/ViewModels/LibraryViewModel.swift` (SHA `a39f0906dfb666fa11877973873c4157da77192c`), then `ExportSheetContractTests` was run against the reverted pair — it reproduced as a **genuine compile error**, not just a failing assertion: the reverted `LibraryViewModel.swift` still called `ExportRequest(..., jpegQuality: quality)`, which no longer compiles against the already-landed generalized `ExportRequest`. Restored via `git stash apply` (not `pop`) and dropped the entry afterward; `git stash list` was empty again before continuing.
- **Tests added**: `Tests/RawProcessingCoreTests/ExportFormatTests.swift` (8, new), `ExportResizingTests.swift` (8, new), `ExifRetentionPolicyTests.swift` (5, new), `ExportMetadataBuilderTests.swift` (5, new), `PhotoExportTests.swift` (formerly `JPEGExportTests.swift`, 20 pre-existing + 11 new: PNG/TIFF/HEIC round-trip, explicit-JPEG-matches-default, capability-guard reject-before-write and unrelated-gap-is-fine, max-width resize + no-cap-keeps-native, DPI written + no-DPI-request-still-valid, 16-bit/8-bit TIFF + bit-depth-ignored-for-JPEG, EXIF preserveAll/removeAll round-trip, full-quality-decode-recorder), `Tests/LumaHarborAppTests/ExportSheetContractTests.swift` (9, new: option labels, quality/bit-depth gating, format/bit-depth/EXIF picker coverage, unsupported-format visible marking + disabled action, RAW safety copy, distinct in-progress/finished state, failure-surfaces-as-alert, every collected option reaches `MacExportOptions`).
- **Verification**: full `swift test` — 1212 executed, 9 skipped (unchanged fixture-dependent baseline), 0 failures (up from 1161 before this task). `git diff --check` — clean. Privacy scan (`rg -n "/Users/|/Volumes/|/private/|<TEAM_ID>|teamIdentifier:|DEVELOPMENT_TEAM"` over the diff and every new untracked file) — no hits. `swift build` — clean. `xcodebuild -project Apps/LumaHarborPad.xcodeproj -scheme LumaHarborPad -destination 'generic/platform=iOS' -derivedDataPath /private/tmp/LumaHarbor-Phase1Task4-DerivedData CODE_SIGNING_ALLOWED=NO build` — `** BUILD SUCCEEDED **`, confirming the shared `RawProcessingCore` export API changes don't break the iPad app (confirmed via `rg` first: no iPad `.swiftpm` file references the export API at all, so this is a belt-and-suspenders check, not one closing a real usage gap). This worktree's own `Apps/LumaHarborPad.xcodeproj/project.pbxproj` remained untouched throughout (not the file this round's own build ran against).
- **Not in scope, per the task's own boundary**: batch export, rename templates (original filename/sequence/date/preset name/virtual copy name), watermarking, color-space choice beyond sRGB, and same-name collision handling (already existed via the pre-existing `UniqueFilenameResolver`, reused unchanged for every format).
- **Not started**: Task 5 (Phase 1 verification and handoff) of `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-02-awayphotoraweditor-parity-phase1.md`.
- **Next action**: continue with Task 5 (run the plan's own full verification/report steps and request independent review) from this same branch/worktree. Do not push, merge, rebase, remove this worktree, or commit any signing setting without explicit user authorization.

## Phase 1 Task 3 (2026-09-02, Claude, TDD, in this worktree/branch)

- **Status**: `DONE`. Branch `codex/awayphotoraweditor-parity-phase1`, HEAD `53a8a81a8ea637ff70cf1cb32d65a2d9be192674`, one commit ahead of `5bbeb06` (Task 2, already on this branch before this round started). Base branch `main` at `8a400edb0f07082d157abb28b9c688d18db98f34`.
- **What changed**: the Mac inspector (`Sources/LumaHarborApp/Views/InspectorView.swift`) now groups every existing adjustment into five labeled panels — Basic, Color, Curve, Detail, Effects (design spec §6.3) — instead of one flat list. Basic keeps mounting the existing, already-tested `BasicAdjustmentPanel` unchanged (it already covers every `AdjustmentKind`, confirmed by the pre-existing `BasicAdjustmentPanelModelTests`). Four new panels were added to `Sources/AdjustmentUI/`: `ColorAdjustmentPanel` (all eight HSL bands), `DetailAdjustmentPanel` (sharpening + noise reduction), `EffectsAdjustmentPanel` (vignette + grain), and `CurveAdjustmentPanel` (advanced tone curve identity status + a visible, disableable Reset button — a full draggable curve graph is out of scope for this task per the plan's own "if not finished, record as `NOT RUN`" allowance, since `AdvancedToneCurve`'s only current source is style/XMP import, not direct editing). A new shared `AdjustmentSliderRow` reproduces `BasicAdjustmentPanel`'s own double-click/context-menu reset gesture for every new row, so every adjustment (not just the original ten) has the same visible reset affordance. `splitToning` is deliberately left unexposed: the design spec's own §6.3 group list omits it from Color/Curve/Detail/Effects, so this is a scope decision, not an oversight.
- **Model change**: `EditorSession` gained `updateAdjustments(_ transform: (inout PhotoAdjustments) -> Void)`, a general entry point for the sub-struct fields (`hsl`, `sharpening`, `noiseReduction`, `vignette`, `grain`, `advancedToneCurve`) that have no `AdjustmentKind` case of their own and so can't go through the existing `setAdjustment(_:to:)`. It goes through the exact same undo/autosave path — `history.record` (a no-op when the transform doesn't change anything) followed by the same `didChangeAdjustments()` every other edit path uses. No new sidecar schema was added; every field it touches already existed in `PhotoAdjustments`.
- **TDD**: RED confirmed by writing the new tests and contract tests before `updateAdjustments` or the four new panel types existed — `swift build`/`swift test` failed to compile (`error: value of type 'EditorSession' has no member 'updateAdjustments'`) until the model change landed, and the new `AdjustmentUITests`/`LumaHarborAppTests` contract tests failed to find their target source files until the panels were added. One contract test (`testColorPanelCoversAllEightHSLBandsWithHueSaturationAndLuminanceRows`) was corrected mid-round: its first draft assumed literal `L10n.t("Red")`-style call sites, but the actual (equally correct) implementation looks up labels from a keyed array via `L10n.t(band.labelKey)` — the test was loosened to check for the key string's presence rather than the exact call-site shape, without weakening what it actually verifies (every band/field is present, localized, and built from the shared row).
- **Tests added**: `Tests/EditorCoreTests/EditorSessionEditingTests.swift` (+4: `updateAdjustments` creates one undo entry, is a no-op with an unchanged value or no open photo, and can reach every sub-struct the panels need), `Tests/EditorCoreTests/EditorSessionDocumentPersistenceTests.swift` (+1: `updateAdjustments` autosaves and round-trips through a real `PhotoDocumentStore` exactly like `setAdjustment` does, RAW bytes unchanged), `Tests/AdjustmentUITests/AdjustmentGroupPanelsContractTests.swift` (new file, 5 tests: shared row's reset-gesture contract, Color/Detail/Effects field coverage, Curve panel's Reset button), `Tests/LumaHarborAppTests/InspectorAdjustmentGroupsContractTests.swift` (new file, 3 tests: every group has a localized header and actually mounts its panel in `InspectorView`, every new panel source file exists).
- **Verification**: full `swift test` — 1161 executed, 9 skipped (unchanged fixture-dependent baseline), 0 failures. `git diff --check` — clean. Privacy scan (`rg -n "/Users/|/Volumes/|/private/|<TEAM_ID>|teamIdentifier:|DEVELOPMENT_TEAM"` over `git diff`) — no hits. `swift build` — clean. `xcodebuild -project Apps/LumaHarborPad.xcodeproj -scheme LumaHarborPad -destination 'generic/platform=iOS' -derivedDataPath /private/tmp/LumaHarbor-Phase1Task3-DerivedData CODE_SIGNING_ALLOWED=NO build` — `** BUILD SUCCEEDED **`, confirming the new `EditorCore`/`AdjustmentUI` public API doesn't break the iPad app that also depends on those targets (no iPad UI file was touched or needed to be). This worktree's own `Apps/LumaHarborPad.xcodeproj/project.pbxproj` was and remains clean (`git status` showed no dirty signing file here) — nothing was preserved-but-uncommitted from this round.
- **`NOT RUN` / follow-up, explicitly not claimed as `PASS`**: precision/keyboard-adjustable input for any slider (every row still uses a plain `Slider`, matching `BasicAdjustmentPanel`'s own existing pattern — no text-field precision entry anywhere in this inspector yet); an interactive curve-graph editor for `AdvancedToneCurve` (this round only exposes identity status + Reset); any real-device/Simulator manual check of the new grouped panels' visual layout (this environment has no way to drive the Mac app's UI or take screenshots).
- **Not started**: Task 4 (single-photo export options foundation) and Task 5 (Phase 1 verification/handoff) of `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-09-02-awayphotoraweditor-parity-phase1.md`. No geometry, batch, preset-schema, or local-retouching work was touched, per the plan's own out-of-scope list for this task.
- **Next action**: continue with Task 4 (single-photo export options foundation) from this same branch/worktree, or hand off for independent review of Task 3 first. Do not push, merge, rebase, remove this worktree, or commit any signing setting without explicit user authorization.

### `claude/ipad-ui-polish-finish` review fixes + Task 3 + Task 4 (2026-09-02, Claude, TDD, from the isolated worktree `.worktrees/claude-ipad-ui-polish-finish`)

- **Review fix** (`aad02df93d297092cbc6af183f72189ccb52a8b7`, `fix: keep iPad library operation state scoped`): fixed two findings from the Task 1/Task 2 handoff review before starting Task 3.
  1. `LibraryBrowserSession.addSource`/`relinkSource`/`removeSource` used `defer { operationState = .idle }`, which could clobber a newer, still in-flight operation's state (e.g. a `relinkSource` started after an `addSource`, once the `addSource` call finishes). Replaced with a `clearOperationState(ifStill:)` helper mirroring `scanSource(_:)`'s own existing completion guard, so only the operation that actually set the current state may clear it. RED confirmed by stashing only `Sources/EditorCore/LibraryBrowserSession.swift` back to its pre-fix committed content (`git stash push -u -m "part1-red-check-librarybrowsersession-src-only"`, SHA `258978a0660f5e01ff8a4bbbeceea6d1568be0dc`) and running the new `testAddSourceCompletionDoesNotClobberNewerReconnectOperationState` test, which failed as expected (`operationState` was `.idle` instead of the still in-flight `.reconnectingSource(...)`), then restoring via `git stash apply` (not `pop`) and dropping the entry.
  2. `PadLibrarySidebar` kept its own local `reconnectingSourceID`/`removingSourceID` `@State` and mounted its own `PadLibraryProgressOverlay`, duplicating `PadLibraryView`'s global `operationState`-driven overlay at regular (non-compact) width, where both views are mounted at once in an `HStack`. Removed the sidebar's local state/overlay entirely; `LibraryBrowserSession.operationState` is now the single source of truth, and the sidebar's relink/remove handlers just call `library.relinkSource`/`removeSource` directly. Replaced the two `PadLibraryAccessibilityContractTests` that asserted the removed local state (`testRemovingSourceShowsVisibleProgressAndRawSafetyCopy`, `testReconnectingSourceShowsVisibleProgressAndIdentityCopy`) with `testRemovingSourceCallsRemoveSourceAfterConfirmation`, `testReconnectingSourceCallsRelinkSourceAfterPickingAFolder`, and a new `testSidebarDoesNotDuplicateGlobalOperationOverlay`. RED confirmed the same stash-and-restore way, isolated to `PadLibrarySidebar.swift` (SHA `4e76c4be5167970c617e91db8a5d8bb4fb7c49fc`): the new duplicate-overlay test failed with 3 assertion failures against the pre-fix sidebar.
  - `LibraryBrowserSessionTests`: 43 executed, 0 failures (42 pre-existing + 1 new). `PadLibraryAccessibilityContractTests`: 30 executed, 0 failures (29 pre-existing, 2 replaced, 3 new — net +1). Changed exactly: `Sources/EditorCore/LibraryBrowserSession.swift`, `Tests/EditorCoreTests/LibraryBrowserSessionTests.swift`, `Apps/LumaHarborPad.swiftpm/Sources/LumaHarborPadApp/PadLibrarySidebar.swift`, `Tests/AdjustmentUITests/PadLibraryAccessibilityContractTests.swift`.
- **Task 3** (`ebdbeb0a245b6af419081a37718703bebb6726c8`, `feat: clarify iPad editor save failure feedback`): `PadEditorView.saveStatusIndicator`'s `.failed` branch changed from a bare `Not saved` label to `Save failed` plus the underlying failure message plus a fixed RAW-safety hint (`Your RAW original was not changed.`); `Saved`/`Unsaved`/`Saving…` unchanged. The pre-existing `Not saved` key was kept (still used by the Mac app's `LumaHarborApp/Views/EditorView.swift`, confirmed via `rg` before deciding not to remove it). Added `Save failed` and `Your RAW original was not changed.` to both `Localizable.strings` files. RED confirmed by stashing `PadEditorView.swift` back to its pre-fix content (SHA `29b63d21f34a3eb6d92419c35c2adaa35f0499af`) and running the new `PadLibraryCompositionContractTests/testPadEditorUsesSaveFailedCopyAndRawSafetyHint` (3 assertion failures), then restoring via `git stash apply` and dropping the entry. `PadLibraryCompositionContractTests`: 7 executed, 0 failures (6 pre-existing + 1 new). `EditorSessionDocumentPersistenceTests/testSaveFailureIsNeverReportedAsSaved`: already existed, already passed (no `EditorSession.SaveState` behavior change was needed — it already carried `.failed(String)`). ### `claude/ipad-ui-polish-finish` Codex review P1 fix (2026-09-02, Claude, TDD, from the isolated worktree `.worktrees/claude-ipad-ui-polish-finish`)

- **Codex review finding (P1, blocking)**: reviewing this branch, Codex found that the review fix above (`aad02df`, item 2) removed `PadLibrarySidebar`'s local overlay *unconditionally*. That's correct at regular width (where `PadLibraryView`'s global `operationState` overlay already covers the sidebar, mounted side by side in one `HStack`), but at compact width `PadLibraryView` presents the sidebar inside a `.sheet`, which sits visually *above* that global overlay. With no sidebar-local overlay left, a reconnect/remove kicked off from inside that sheet was invisible until the user dismissed the sheet — a regression against spec §5.2/§5.3's "any operation longer than a tap must have visible text" requirement, exactly on the two most consequential operations (reconnect, remove).
- **Fix** (`bb63989d091bbcc1e2025c3161c97376abfff22e`, `fix: show sidebar operation overlay inside the compact sheet`): `PadLibrarySidebar` now takes a `showsOperationOverlay: Bool` the caller controls. `PadLibraryView` passes `false` for the regular-width `HStack` call site (unchanged behavior — global overlay still covers it) and `true` for the compact-width `.sheet` call site (new — closes the gap). The sidebar's own overlay content is derived from a private `operationOverlayContent` computed property that switches on `library.operationState` — the exact same `LibraryBrowserSession.operationState` `PadLibraryView`'s own `activeLibraryOverlay` reads — not a second, independent state; `reconnectingSourceID`/`removingSourceID` were not reintroduced. RED confirmed by running the new `PadLibraryAccessibilityContractTests/testSidebarOperationOverlayIsOnlyEnabledForTheCompactSheet` against the pre-fix sidebar/view (4 assertion failures: no `showsOperationOverlay` flag, no conditional overlay, no `operationState`-derived content, no differentiated call sites), then implementing until GREEN.
- `PadLibraryAccessibilityContractTests`: 30 executed, 0 failures (29 pre-existing minus the replaced `testSidebarDoesNotDuplicateGlobalOperationOverlay` plus the new `testSidebarOperationOverlayIsOnlyEnabledForTheCompactSheet` — net even; two other pre-existing tests' doc comments were updated for accuracy, not their assertions). Full `swift test`: 1125 executed, 9 skipped, 0 failures, confirmed on two consecutive clean runs (one earlier run showed 1 failure that did not reproduce on retry or on either of the two clean runs after — not traced further, since it did not touch any file this fix changed; flagged here rather than silently discarded). `git diff --check`: clean. Privacy scan (`/Users/|/Volumes/|/private/|<TEAM_ID>|teamIdentifier:|DEVELOPMENT_TEAM`) over this fix's own diff: no hits. Changed exactly: `Apps/LumaHarborPad.swiftpm/Sources/LumaHarborPadApp/PadLibrarySidebar.swift`, `Apps/LumaHarborPad.swiftpm/Sources/LumaHarborPadApp/PadLibraryView.swift`, `Tests/AdjustmentUITests/PadLibraryAccessibilityContractTests.swift`.
- `xcodebuild -project Apps/LumaHarborPad.xcodeproj -scheme LumaHarborPad -destination 'generic/platform=iOS' -derivedDataPath /private/tmp/LumaHarbor-iPadUIPolish-P1Fix-DerivedData CODE_SIGNING_ALLOWED=NO build` — `** BUILD SUCCEEDED **`, re-run for this specific fix (not merely carried forward from `ebdbeb0`/`2443061`); this compiled the new `showsOperationOverlay` stored property, the new `operationOverlayContent` computed property, and both updated `PadLibrarySidebar(` call sites in `PadLibraryView.swift` through `swiftc` for `arm64-apple-ios17.0` — the class of error source-parsing contract tests cannot catch.
- **`NOT RUN`**: every real-device/Simulator manual check (plan §9) remains `NOT RUN`, now also covering whether the compact-sheet overlay is actually visible on hardware.
